- '开源最佳/发布日不晚于它的闭源最佳' → 注明这衡量的是'开源追平同期闭源到什么程度';HLE/OSWorld/METR/Terminal-Bench 的开源最佳是 2025-11 到 2026-02 的旧模型,因为 Epoch 没测新开源模型;对当前闭源最佳:HLE 0.45、FrontierSWE 0.40、METR 0.05、Terminal-Bench 0.62
- '长程 agentic 0.27–0.78' → 修正 APEX 后为 0.27–0.87,区间与知识类(0.94–0.98)仍分离,但 APEX 不宜作为'低'的例证
- OSWorld 用的是旧版 OSWorld(Epoch os_world_external),不是 OSWorld 2
- AA '3–6 点' → 原文只写 6 点(vs GPT-5.5 60);3 点是对 Gemini 3.1 Pro / Opus 4.7(57)自算的,需注明
- Intelligence Index 'v4.3' → 页面上未见版本号,标【未验证】;并注明 Opus 5.5 是 'max with fallback',Gemini 4 Argon 是 high 档
- Ihle 的 8–10/4–6 个月 → 注明是开源 vs 闭源(非中美)、backward-looking、部分'私有'基准并非完全私有、作者自承第三方 provider 降级可能放大差距
- Epoch 7 个月 → 注明是中国 vs 美国(非开源 vs 闭源),截至 2026-01-02,基于 ECI 当时版本

**核对过程**:(a) 复算方法:用 model_metadata.csv 的 accessibility 字段,以 'Open weights*'(含 restricted/non-commercial,例如 Kimi K3 属 non-commercial)为开源;每个基准取开源最高分,再取发布日不晚于它的闭源最高分求比值。结果:GPQA kimi-k3 0.931 / gpt-5.4-pro 0.946 = 0.984;SWE-bench Verified glm-5.2 0.787 / opus-4.7 0.835 = 0.943;DeepSWE kimi-k3 0.685 / gpt-5.6-sol 0.727 = 0.943;OSWorld kimi-k2.5 63.3 / opus-4.5 66.3 = 0.955;FrontierMath T1-3 v2 kimi-k3 0.722 / gpt-5.6-sol 0.891 = 0.810;APEX-Agents glm-5.3-flash 0.528 / gemini-3.7-flash 0.678 = 0.779;Terminal-Bench glm-5 0.524 / opus-4.6 0.798 = 0.657;HLE kimi-k2.5 0.244 / gemini-3-pro-preview 0.375 = 0.650;FrontierSWE kimi-k3 0.259 / fable-5 0.470 = 0.551;METR TH kimi-k2-thinking 54.2 min / gpt-5 203 min = 0.267。原表数字全部复现。但有三处口径问题。第一,APEX:mimo-v2.6-pro(0.595,2026-09-21)在 Epoch 元数据里没有 accessibility,所以被排除;而 AA 排行页原文写着 'MiMo-V2.6-Pro is the highest-ranked open weights model with an Intelligence Index score of 46'。算作开源的话,0.595 / claude-fable-5-1 0.686 = 0.867,APEX 应在 0.87 左右而不是 0.78。第二,'发布日不晚于'这个口径偏宽:HLE、OSWorld、METR、Terminal-Bench 的开源最佳都是 2025-11 到 2026-02 的旧模型(新开源模型 Epoch 没测),拿去和同期闭源比,反映的是覆盖度而不是当前差距。换成对当前闭源最佳,比值是 HLE 0.445、FrontierSWE 0.395、METR 0.052、Terminal-Bench 0.619、APEX 0.642(或 0.72)。第三,OSWorld 用的是旧版 os_world 文件,不是 osworld_2。另外各基准的推理档位和 harness 不统一(Terminal-Bench 取了不同 agent 的最高分)。(b) AA 原文逐字核对:'tie as the leading open weights models on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index at 54';GPT-5.5 (xhigh) 60;'just one year ago the highest-scoring open weights model was DeepSeek V3 0324 which achieved 22 ... ~13 points below ... Claude 3.7 Sonnet (Reasoning) at 35';'The gap to proprietary remains wide on the hardest reasoning and agentic coding evaluations.';'The top 10 open weights models on the Intelligence Index are all from China-based AI labs.' 都核对无误。'3–6 点'是自己推出来的:对 GPT-5.5 是 6 点,对 Gemini 3.1 Pro 和 Opus 4.7(都是 57)是 3 点,原文只给了 6 点。2026-10-12 排行页上的数字:Claude Opus 5.5 (max with fallback) 58、GPT-6 Astra (max) 53、Gemini 4 Argon (high) 53、MiMo-V2.6-Pro 46、Kimi K3 (max) 44,数字吻合;但 'v4.3' 这个版本号在页面上找不到(HTML 里只出现 V4.1 等字样),版本号只能算未验证。Opus 是 'with fallback',Gemini 4 Argon 是 high 档不是 max;排第 4 的 Muse Wonder Full (56) 比 GPT-6/Gemini 高。(c) Ihle 原文(Håvard Tveit Ihle,28 May 2026)逐字核对:'on private benchmarks ... open models are roughly 8-10 months behind the closed frontier, while for public benchmarks the gap is roughly 4-6 months. We also find that the gap was smallest around the time of DeepSeek R1, in Jan 2025, and since then the gap has been growing.' 吻合。限定:这是开源对闭源的比较,不是中国对美国;作者自己说数字是 backward-looking;有 'several of the “private” benchmarks not being fully private' 和 'Provider degradation may inflate the gap' 两条警示;作者本人运营 WeirdML,而 WeirdML 是被纳入的私有基准之一。(d) Epoch 原文逐字核对(Luke Emberson,Jan. 2, 2026):'Since 2023, every model at the frontier of AI capabilities, as measured by the Epoch Capabilities Index, has been developed in the United States. ... trailed US capabilities by an average of seven months, with a minimum gap of four months and a maximum gap of 14.' 吻合。这是国别比较,不是开源对闭源;ECI 每次重拟合会漂移,该数字截至 2026-01。反证:外部检索到的二手报道显示 NIST/CAISI 称差距约 8 个月,也有'差 3%'的说法,说明差距的口径分歧很大,本文未逐一回一手核实。

来源:https://epoch.ai/data/benchmark_data.zip (gpqa_diamond.csv, swe_bench_verified.csv, deepswe_external.csv, os_world_external.csv, frontiermath_tiers_1_3_v2.csv, frontiermath.csv, apex_agents_external.csv, terminalbench_external.csv, hle_external.csv, frontierswe_external.csv, metr_time_horizons_external.csv, model_metadata.csv); https://epoch.ai/data/all_ai_models.csv; https://www.artificialanalysis.ai/articles/recent-open-weights-model-launches (curl 原文); https://artificialanalysis.ai/leaderboards/models (curl + fetch); https://www.greaterwrong.com/posts/rJcCrXyEsJKmmDpWG/how-far-behind-are-open-models (curl 原文); https://epoch.ai/data-insights/us-vs-china-eci (curl 原文)

## 票 2:CORRECTED(单源已核)

**修正后表述**:按 Epoch 2026-10 数据,开源最佳与同期或更早闭源最佳的比值:GPQA 0.98、SWE-bench Verified 0.94、DeepSWE 0.94、OSWorld 0.95(开源点为 2026-01 的 Kimi K2.5)、APEX-Agents 约 0.87(MiMo-V2.6-Pro vs Claude Fable 5.1)、Terminal-Bench 2.0 同 harness 约 0.83、FrontierMath T1-3 0.81、FrontierSWE 0.55(Kimi K3 0.259 vs Claude Fable 5 0.470)。HLE、METR 的开源数据过旧,不宜并列。分化存在,但集中在最难的长程工程/数学任务上,并非'agentic 一律 0.3–0.8'。AA 2026-04-30 称最佳开源(54)距领先闭源 3–6 点(距 GPT-5.5 的 60 为 6 点),一年前约 13 点,且'the gap to proprietary remains wide on the hardest reasoning and agentic coding evaluations'。各方对开源落后时长的估计不一:Ihle 称私有基准 8–10 个月、公开基准 4–6 个月且在扩大;Epoch 按 ECI 测得 2026 年以来约 4 个月;Epoch 另测得中国模型平均落后美国前沿约 7 个月,且截至 2026-10 ECI 前沿仍全部是美国模型。

**口径修正**:
- (a) APEX 0.78 → 0.87:MiMo-V2.6-Pro 是 MIT 开源权重,0.595 对比 Fable 5.1 0.686;Epoch 元数据漏分类
- (a) TB2.0 0.66 → 跨 harness 比值(Terminus 2 vs ForgeCode);同为 Terminus 2 时 0.524/0.629≈0.83
- (a) HLE 0.65 → 开源仅有 2026-01 的 Kimi K2.5 数据点,属陈旧配对;AA 2026-04 同期约 0.77–0.82
- (a) METR 0.27 → 配对是 Kimi K2 Thinking(2025-11)vs GPT-5(2025-08),且时间地平线是对数量,不能与准确率比值并列,需单独说明或删除
- (a) '饱和/知识 0.94–0.98 vs 长程 agentic 0.27–0.78' → DeepSWE、OSWorld 是 agentic 却为 0.94–0.95;修正后低值主要剩 FrontierSWE 0.55 和 FrontierMath 0.81,分化有,但幅度明显小于原文
- (a) Kimi K3 需注明为 non-commercial 开源权重;每个比值标注开源模型日期
- (b) '距 GPT-5.5(60)3–6 点' → 距 GPT-5.5 为 6 点,距 Gemini 3.1 Pro / Opus 4.7(57)为 3 点
- (b) 10-12 榜单:第 2 名是 Sonnet 5.5 / Muse Wonder Full 56,不是 GPT-6 Astra;Anthropic 分数带 'with fallback';v4.3 版本号未在页面核到
- (c) 保留,但须并列 Epoch 2026-05(按 ECI 落后 4 个月)与 AISI cyber(4–7 个月且在缩小)两个反证
- (d) HOLDS(原文 2026-01 口径);复算当前数据平均约 7.5–8 个月,区间随重拟合漂移;前沿全美国截至 2026-10 仍成立

**核对过程**:(a) 复算:用 eci_scores.csv 的 Accessibility group 经 processed_data_for_eci 的 model_version 映射给每条基准行分类开/闭;取开源最高分,再取发布日 <= 该开源模型的闭源最高分。十个比值全部复现:GPQA 0.931/0.946=0.984,SWE-V 0.787/0.835=0.943,DeepSWE 0.685/0.727=0.943(Kimi K3 vs GPT-5.6 Sol),OSWorld 63.3/66.3=0.955,FM T1-3 0.722/0.891=0.81,APEX 0.528/0.678=0.779,TB2.0 0.524/0.798=0.657,HLE 0.244/0.375=0.65,FrontierSWE 0.259/0.470=0.551,METR 54.2/203.0=0.267。但反证发现以下问题:(1) APEX:Epoch 元数据没给 mimo-v2.6-pro(2026-09-21,0.595)分类,而它是 MIT 许可的开源权重。纳入后,开源最佳=0.595,对比发布日 <=09-21 的闭源最佳 Claude Fable 5.1 0.686,比值=0.87,不是 0.78。(2) TB2.0 的 harness 混杂:GLM-5 的 0.524 用的是中性 harness Terminus 2,Opus 4.6 的 0.798 用的是第三方专用 agent ForgeCode(run date 2026-03-12)。同用 Terminus 2 时 Opus 4.6 为 0.629,比值≈0.83。(3) 数据陈旧:有几个基准的开源最佳数据点早就过时,因为 Epoch 后来没再收录新开源模型,所以算出的比值反映的是收录时间,不是当前差距。HLE 的开源点只有 Kimi K2.5(2026-01);AA 2026-04 显示开源前三 HLE 34–36%,对比 GPT-5.5 44%、Gemini 3.1 Pro 45%,比值约 0.77–0.82。OSWorld 同样只有 Kimi K2.5(2026-01)。TB 只有 GLM-5(2026-02)。METR 只有 Kimi K2 Thinking(2025-11),配对的是 GPT-5(2025-08)。(4) METR 时间地平线是对数尺度量,其比值不能与准确率比值并列;准确率接近 1 时比值被机械压缩(天花板效应)。(5) 分组标签有误:DeepSWE 0.94 和 OSWorld 0.95 本身就是 agentic 基准,却落在“饱和/知识”区间。修正后唯一稳健的长程低值是 FrontierSWE 0.55(另有 FM 0.81)。(6) Kimi K3 是 Open weights (non-commercial),开源定义需注明。(b) AA 4/30 文章逐字核到:'tie as the leading open weights models ... at 54'、'GPT-5.5 (xhigh) at 60'、'within 3-6 points of the leading proprietary models'、'The gap to proprietary remains wide on the hardest reasoning and agentic coding evaluations'、'The top 10 open weights models on the Intelligence Index are all from China-based AI labs'、DeepSeek V3 0324=22、约 13 点(当时领先者 Claude 3.7 Sonnet 35)。口径错误在于 3–6 点是对多个领先者而言:对 GPT-5.5 是 6 点,对 Gemini 3.1 Pro 和 Opus 4.7(57)是 3 点。10-12 排行榜核到 Opus 5.5 (max with fallback) 58、GPT-6 Astra (max) 53、Gemini 4 Argon (high) 53、MiMo-V2.6-Pro 46、Kimi K3 (max) 44。但只列这几个是挑选:第 2 名是 Claude Sonnet 5.5 56 和 Muse Wonder Full 56,Fable 5.1 为 53,GPT-6.1 Sol 为 52。Anthropic 条目带 'with fallback' 标注,页面未定义其含义。页面未显示版本号 v4.3,无法核实。(c) Ihle(Håvard Tveit Ihle,2026-05-28)逐字核到 8–10 / 4–6 个月及 R1 那句,方法为 17 个基准、约 110 个数据点。作者自承有 winner's-curse 偏差、早期数据部分为自报、'private' 并非完全私有;HN 批评被测开源模型偏旧。反证:Epoch 2026-05-29 按 ECI 测得自 2026-01 起开源落后 4 个月,差距'slightly widened';UK AISI 网络安全评测为 4–7 个月,并称比 2025 年的 6–10 个月缩小。(d) Epoch 2026-01-02(Luke Emberson)逐字核到 7 个月、最小 4、最大 14、'every model at the frontier ... Epoch Capabilities Index'。用当前 eci_scores.csv 复算(逐日取中国最佳,求美国首次达到其分数减 1 的日期):2023 年以来平均约 7.8 个月,范围 1.1–18.1;2026 年平均 7.5 个月,当前约 8.2 个月(Kimi K3 157.45)。区间与原文 4–14 不同,源于 ECI 重拟合漂移和方法近似,平均值稳健。截至 2026-10 的 ECI 前沿仍全部为美国模型,Axios 2026-07 'China just erased America's AI lead' 的说法在 ECI 上不成立。

来源:https://epoch.ai/data/benchmark_data.zip (gpqa_diamond.csv, swe_bench_verified.csv, deepswe_external.csv, os_world_external.csv, frontiermath_tiers_1_3_v2.csv, apex_agents_external.csv, terminalbench_external.csv, hle_external.csv, frontierswe_external.csv, metr_time_horizons_external.csv, epoch_capabilities_index/eci_scores.csv + processed_data_for_eci.csv); https://www.artificialanalysis.ai/articles/recent-open-weights-model-launches; https://artificialanalysis.ai/leaderboards/models (2026-10-12); https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/rJcCrXyEsJKmmDpWG/how-far-behind-are-open-models; https://epoch.ai/data-insights/us-vs-china-eci; Epoch Data Insight 'Open models lag state-of-the-art closed models by 4 months' (2026-05-29)

## 票 3:CORRECTED(单源已核)

**修正后表述**:按 Epoch 基准数据(2026-10 下载;开源 = Epoch 标注的 open weights;分母 = 发布日不晚于该开源模型的闭源最佳)自算:GPQA 0.98、SWE-bench Verified 0.94、DeepSWE 0.94、OSWorld 0.95,知识/饱和类基准开源接近追平;FrontierMath T1-3 0.81、APEX-Agents 0.78(若把 MiMo-V2.6-Pro 算作开源则为 0.87)、Terminal-Bench 2.0 同 harness 0.83(跨 harness 为 0.66)、FrontierSWE 0.55,难的 agentic 基准差距明显更大;HLE、METR 的开源数据停在 2025 年末到 2026 年初,不宜下结论。Artificial Analysis 2026-04-30:Kimi K2.6、MiMo V2.5 Pro 得 54,距 GPT-5.5(60)6 点、距 Gemini 3.1 Pro 和 Opus 4.7(57)3 点,一年前差约 13 点;但“在最难的推理与 agentic 编码评测上差距仍大”,前 10 开源全部来自中国实验室。2026-10-12 的 v4.3:Opus 5.5(max with fallback)58、GPT-6 Astra 53、Gemini 4 Argon 53、开源最高 MiMo-V2.6-Pro 46、Kimi K3 44(与四月版本不可比)。Ihle(2026-05-28)估计开源模型在私有基准上落后 8–10 个月、公开基准 4–6 个月,差距在 2025 年 1 月 R1 前后最小、此后扩大(作者自承部分私有基准不完全私有)。Epoch(2026-01-02)以 ECI 衡量:2023 年以来中国模型平均落后美国前沿 7 个月(4–14),前沿模型全部出自美国。

**口径修正**:
- (a) Terminal-Bench 2.0 0.66(GLM-5 Terminus 2 对比 Opus 4.6 ForgeCode)→ 同 harness(Terminus 2)为 0.524/0.629 = 0.83;跨 harness 的 0.66 须注明或删去
- (a) APEX-Agents 0.78 → 若按 AA 把 MiMo-V2.6-Pro 算作开源(Epoch 元数据漏标),为 0.595/0.686 = 0.87;须注明开源集合定义
- (a) HLE 0.65 / OSWorld 0.95 / METR 0.27 → 开源最佳来自 2025-11~2026-01 的老模型,反映的是 Epoch 数据覆盖缺口;METR 是分钟数比值,与正确率比值不可并列
- (a) “饱和 0.94–0.98,长程 0.27–0.78”→ 改为“知识/饱和类 0.94–0.98;较难的 agentic/数学类约 0.55–0.87(随 harness 和开源定义变化);METR 时间跨度另算,且开源数据陈旧”
- (a) 分母口径:写明“发布日不晚于该开源模型的闭源最佳”;若按当前闭源最佳,比值显著更低(HLE 0.45、FrontierSWE 0.40)
- (b) “最佳开源距 GPT-5.5(60)3–6 点”→ “距 GPT-5.5 6 点,距 Gemini 3.1 Pro 和 Opus 4.7(57)3 点”
- (b) v4.3 数值核对无误;补充标注 Opus 5.5 为 'max with fallback' 档;v4.3 内开源/闭源差为 12 点,不得与四月 v4.x 的 6 点直接比较
- (c) 逐字属实;补充限定:对象为全体开源模型(非专指中国),backward-looking;作者自承部分“私有”基准并不完全私有,服务商降级可能夸大差距;另有 Epoch/Mozilla 约 4 个月的估计(二手,未核)
- (d) 逐字属实;补上“以 ECI 衡量”和“截至 2026-01”

**核对过程**:(a) 复算方法:开源 = Epoch model_metadata accessibility 以 'Open weights' 开头(含 restricted/non-commercial,如 Kimi K3 为 non-commercial);闭源 = API access/Hosted/Limited/Unreleased;分子 = 开源最高分,分母 = 发布日 ≤ 该开源模型发布日的闭源最高分。十个比值全部复现:GPQA 0.984(Kimi K3 0.931 vs GPT-5.4 Pro 0.946)、SWE-V 0.943(GLM-5.2 0.787 vs Opus 4.7 0.835)、DeepSWE 0.943(Kimi K3 0.685 vs GPT-5.6 Sol 0.727)、OSWorld 0.955(Kimi K2.5 63.3 vs Opus 4.5 66.3)、FrontierMath T1-3 0.810、APEX-Agents 0.779(GLM-5.3-Flash 0.528 vs Gemini 3.7 Flash 0.678)、Terminal-Bench 0.657(GLM-5 0.524 vs Opus 4.6 0.798)、HLE 0.650(Kimi K2.5 0.244 vs Gemini 3 Pro 0.375)、FrontierSWE 0.551(Kimi K3 0.259 vs Claude Fable 5 0.470)、METR 0.267(Kimi K2 Thinking 54 min vs GPT-5 203 min)。但口径问题:①Terminal-Bench 2.0 分子分母 harness 不同——GLM-5 用 Terminus 2,Opus 4.6 的 0.798 是 ForgeCode 第三方 agent;同为 Terminus 2 时 Opus 4.6 = 0.629,比值 0.833,不是 0.66。②APEX-Agents:Epoch 元数据里 mimo-v2.6-pro accessibility 为空被漏掉,而 AA 明确称 MiMo-V2.6-Pro 是 'highest-ranked open weights model';计入后开源最佳 0.595(2026-09-21),同期闭源最佳 Claude Fable 5.1 0.686,比值 0.867。③HLE、OSWorld、Terminal-Bench、METR 的开源最佳来自 2025-11~2026-02 的老模型,是数据覆盖缺口(Epoch 未收录较新开源模型的成绩),不能解读为“长程任务开源停滞”;METR 尤甚(开源点停在 2025-11)。且 METR 是分钟数比值,与正确率比值量纲不同,不应放进同一 0.27–0.98 区间并列。④“发布日不晚于”的分母偏宽松;若用全时闭源最佳,比值降到 GPQA 0.97、FrontierMath 0.77、APEX 0.64、HLE 0.45、FrontierSWE 0.40、METR 0.05。(b) AA 4-30 原文逐字:'Kimi K2.6 (Reasoning) and Xiaomi's MiMo V2.5 Pro (Reasoning) tie ... at 54 ... within 3-6 points of the leading proprietary models: OpenAI's GPT-5.5 (xhigh) at 60, and ... Gemini 3.1 Pro Preview and ... Claude Opus 4.7 ... at 57'——6 点是对 GPT-5.5,3 点是对 Gemini 3.1 Pro/Opus 4.7,论断写“距 GPT-5.5 3–6 点”是误读。一年前:DeepSeek V3 0324 22 vs Claude 3.7 Sonnet 35,'~13 points' 属实。'The gap to proprietary remains wide on the hardest reasoning and agentic coding evaluations' 逐字核对(后接 HLE 34-36% vs 44-45%)。'The top 10 open weights models on the Intelligence Index are all from China-based AI labs' 逐字核对。v4.3 当前(AA 首页 2026-10-12):Claude Opus 5.5 (max with fallback) 57.62→58,GPT-6 Astra (max) 52.67→53,Gemini 4 Argon (high) 52.56→53,MiMo-V2.6-Pro 46.32→46,Kimi K3 (max) 43.59→44,全部对得上;注意 Opus 5.5 为 'max with fallback' 档,Gemini 为 high 档;且 Claude Sonnet 5.5 与 Opus 5.5 并列最高。v4.3 下开源-闭源差 58−46=12 点(比四月 v4.x 的 6 点大),文章不得把两版本差距并列成趋势,但也不应隐瞒当前版本内差距是 12 点。(c) Ihle 原文逐字:'on private benchmarks ... open models are roughly 8-10 months behind the closed frontier, while for public benchmarks the gap is roughly 4-6 months. We also find that the gap was smallest around the time of DeepSeek R1, in Jan 2025, and since then the gap has been growing.' 日期 28 May 2026 属实。限定:对象是全部开源模型而非中国模型;是 backward-looking 度量;作者自承 'several of the “private” benchmarks not being fully private',并单列一节称第三方服务商降级 'may inflate the gap'。反证:搜索摘要显示 Epoch 自 2026-01 以来开源最佳平均落后约 4 个月、Mozilla 2026-09 报告约 4.4 个月(仅二手摘要,未核)。(d) Epoch 2026-01-02 原文:'by an average of seven months','a minimum gap of four months and a maximum gap of 14';'Since 2023, every model at the frontier of AI capabilities, as measured by the Epoch Capabilities Index, has been developed in the United States.' 属实,但须保留“以 ECI 衡量”限定,且该文距今 9 个月,ECI 每次重拟合会漂移。

来源:https://epoch.ai/data/benchmark_data.zip (下载于 2026-10-12;model_metadata.csv 的 accessibility 字段 + 10 个基准 csv,python 复算);https://www.artificialanalysis.ai/articles/recent-open-weights-model-launches (curl 原文);https://artificialanalysis.ai/ 首页图表 JSON (v4.3 数值);https://www.greaterwrong.com/posts/rJcCrXyEsJKmmDpWG/how-far-behind-are-open-models (curl 原文);https://epoch.ai/data-insights/us-vs-china-eci

# G23 — 票型 ['CORRECTED', 'CORRECTED', 'HOLDS']

## 票 1:CORRECTED(多源证实)

**修正后表述**:DeepSeek-R1 论文(arXiv 2501.12948)自报:从 R1 蒸馏出的 Qwen-32B 在 AIME 2024 上 pass@1 为 72.6,而在同一 32B 基座上直接做大规模 RL 的 R1-Zero-Qwen-32B 只有 47.0;作者同时写道 'while distillation strategies are both economical and effective, advancing beyond the boundaries of intelligence may still require more powerful base models and larger-scale reinforcement learning'。R1 自报对比 o1-1217:AIME24 79.8 vs 79.2、GPQA Diamond 71.5 vs 75.7、SimpleQA 30.1 vs 47.0。Gudibande 等(ICLR 2024)发现,模仿 ChatGPT 的模型 'close little to none of the gap from the base LM to ChatGPT on tasks that are not heavily supported in the imitation data'(基于 2023 年 ≤13B 的 LLaMA 实验)。Anthropic 2026-02-23 指控 DeepSeek、Moonshot、MiniMax 'generated over 16 million exchanges with Claude through approximately 24,000 fraudulent accounts'(其中 MiniMax 超 1300 万、Moonshot 超 340 万、DeepSeek 约 15 万),称蒸馏能 'in a fraction of the time, and at a fraction of the cost' 获取能力;这是 Anthropic 单方口径,截至检索未见三家公开正式回应。MEM1(arXiv 2506.15841)报告:'when the number of objectives exceeds six, the performance of the SFT model collapses',其表 4 显示 6 个目标时 SFT 已降至 0.088,而 RL 为 1.630。

**口径修正**:
- R1 vs o1-1217 的数字 → 注明是 DeepSeek 论文自报(厂商口径),o1 分数引自 OpenAI
- Gudibande 的'发表于 ICLR 2024?'→ 确认为 ICLR 2024,会议版标题为 'The False Promise of Imitating Proprietary Language Models';结论基于 2023 年 ≤13B 的 LLaMA 模型
- Anthropic 1600 万次 / 2.4 万个账户 → 补充分项:MiniMax 超 1300 万、Moonshot 超 340 万、DeepSeek 约 15 万;不宜笼统写成 DeepSeek 大规模蒸馏
- 被指控方回应 → '截至检索未见三家公开正式回应'(二手来源,未见一手声明);指控为 Anthropic 单方口径
- MEM1 'exceeds six' 引语无误;表 4 显示 6 个目标时 SFT 已降到 0.088,可写成'到 6 个目标左右即崩溃'

**核对过程**:(a) R1 v1 PDF 抽取:Table 6 中 'DeepSeek-R1-Zero-Qwen-32B 47.0 ... DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-32B 72.6'(AIME 2024 pass@1),吻合。逐字引语:'Second, while distillation strategies are both economical and effective, advancing beyond the boundaries of intelligence may still require more powerful base models and larger- scale reinforcement learning.' 吻合。Table 4 o1-1217 对 R1:AIME 2024 79.2 vs 79.8、GPQA Diamond 75.7 vs 71.5、SimpleQA 47.0 vs 30.1,吻合。这些都是 DeepSeek 自报的数据,o1 的数字也是引自 OpenAI 的报告。(b) Gudibande et al. 摘要逐字:'imitation models close little to none of the gap from the base LM to ChatGPT on tasks that are not heavily supported in the imitation data',吻合。论文确实发表于 ICLR 2024(iclr.cc 2024 poster 18877 和 proceedings 都能查到),会议版标题改为 'The False Promise of Imitating Proprietary Language Models'。实验用的是 2023 年的 LLaMA 1.5B–13B 基座,外推到当今规模要谨慎。(c) Anthropic 原文(Feb 23, 2026)逐字:'These labs generated over 16 million exchanges with Claude through approximately 24,000 fraudulent accounts, in violation of our terms of service and regional access restrictions.' 以及 'in a fraction of the time, and at a fraction of the cost, that it would take to develop them independently.' 都吻合。指控对象是 DeepSeek、Moonshot、MiniMax;分项为 MiniMax 'Over 13 million'、Moonshot 'Over 3.4 million'、DeepSeek 'Over 150,000',DeepSeek 不到总量的 1%。关于回应:二手报道(itiger、yicai 等)说三家都没有发正式回应,我未找到任何一家的一手回应声明,'未回应'应写成'截至检索未见公开正式回应'。这是厂商单方指控,归因依据是 IP、请求元数据和基础设施特征,未经独立核实。(d) MEM1 PDF 逐字:'Notably, when the number of objectives exceeds six, the performance of the SFT model collapses',吻合。但 Table 4 数据显示 6 个目标时 SFT 已经只有 0.088(RL 为 1.630),8 个目标 0.027,16 个目标 0.000,也就是在 6 个目标时就已崩溃,正文说的'超过六个'略保守。

来源:https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.12948v1 (pypdf 全文抽取); https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.15717 (pypdf); https://iclr.cc/virtual/2024/poster/18877 与 proceedings.iclr.cc 2024 论文 PDF(检索结果); https://www.anthropic.com/news/detecting-and-preventing-distillation-attacks (curl 原文); https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.15841 (pypdf)

## 票 2:CORRECTED(单源已核)

**修正后表述**:DeepSeek-R1 论文自报:从 R1 蒸馏出的 Qwen-32B 在 AIME 2024 上得 72.6,直接在同一 base 上做大规模 RL 只得 47.0。作者同时写道:'while distillation strategies are both economical and effective, advancing beyond the boundaries of intelligence may still require more powerful base models and larger-scale reinforcement learning.' 更早的 Gudibande 等人(ICLR 2024)发现,在 2023 年的 ChatGPT 对话模仿设置下,模仿模型在模仿数据未覆盖的任务上'close little to none of the gap'。Anthropic 2026-02-23 单方面指控三家中国实验室通过约 2.4 万个欺诈账户与 Claude 产生超过 1600 万次交互,其中 MiniMax 超过 1300 万次、DeepSeek 约 15 万次,被指控方未公开回应。MEM1 显示,在多目标 QA 任务中目标数超过 6 个时,SFT 模型性能崩溃,RL 模型仍保持稳健。

**口径修正**:
- (a) HOLDS;加注 R1 对 o1-1217 的分数为 DeepSeek 自报
- (b) 引语 HOLDS;'ICLR 2024?' → 确认为 ICLR 2024 poster,正式题名为 '...Proprietary Language Models';结论应限定在 2023 年 LLaMA 级 base 与 ChatGPT 对话模仿的设置,R1 推理蒸馏是方向相反的反例
- (c) 16M/24K 引语 HOLDS;'and at a fraction of the cost' 后半句未逐字核全,应降级或只引 'in a fraction of the time';须注明 DeepSeek 只占约 15 万次、MiniMax 超过 1300 万次;被指控方未公开回应,属厂商单方指控
- (d) HOLDS(限定为多目标 QA 任务)

**核对过程**:(a) R1 v1 PDF 表格:DeepSeek-R1-Zero-Qwen-32B AIME 47.0,DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-32B 72.6。原句逐字核到:'while distillation strategies are both economical and effective, advancing beyond the boundaries of intelligence may still require more powerful base models and larger-scale reinforcement learning.' 对比表 AIME 2024 为 79.8 vs 79.2(o1-1217),GPQA Diamond 为 71.5 vs 75.7,SimpleQA 为 30.1 vs 47.0,全部吻合。须注明这是 DeepSeek 自报,o1-1217 数字来自 OpenAI 报告,非独立同 harness 复测。(b) Gudibande 原句逐字核到:'close little to none of the gap from the base LM to ChatGPT on tasks that are not heavily supported in the imitation data.' 论文发表于 ICLR 2024 poster,正式题名为 'The False Promise of Imitating Proprietary Language Models'(arXiv 版用 'LLMs')。反证:2023 年实验用的是 LLaMA 级 base 与 ChatGPT 对话数据,R1-Distill 显示用长推理轨迹蒸馏能有效迁移数学能力,两者结论在推理任务上方向不同,引用时应限定在其实验设置内。(c) Anthropic 2026-02-23 逐字核到:'These labs generated over 16 million exchanges with Claude through approximately 24,000 fraudulent accounts'。'fraction' 一句能核到 '...acquire powerful capabilities from other labs in a fraction of the time';'and at a fraction of the cost' 被抓取截断,未逐字核全。分实验室:DeepSeek 超过 15 万次,Moonshot 超过 340 万次,MiniMax 超过 1300 万次,所以绝大部分来自 MiniMax。被指控三方未见正式回应或否认,只有一家聚合媒体报道其沉默;外部有反驳声音(如 Musk 称其双标)。这属于单方指控,不能当作蒸馏导致能力追平的证据。(d) MEM1 逐字核到:'when the number of objectives exceeds six, the performance of the SFT model collapses, whereas the RL-trained model continues to demonstrate strong robustness ...',语境是多目标 QA 任务上 SFT 与 RL 的对比。

来源:https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.12948v1(pdftotext 逐字 grep);https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.15717;https://iclr.cc/virtual/2024/poster/18877;https://www.anthropic.com/news/detecting-and-preventing-distillation-attacks;https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.15841

## 票 3:HOLDS(单源已核)

**修正后表述**:DeepSeek-R1 论文(arXiv 2501.12948):同一 Qwen-32B 基座上,蒸馏得到的 R1-Distill-Qwen-32B 在 AIME24 得 72.6,直接 RL 的 R1-Zero-Qwen-32B 仅 47.0;作者同时写道,蒸馏“经济且有效”,但要突破智能边界,可能仍需更强的基座和更大规模的 RL。按 R1 自报,其 AIME24 79.8 对 o1-1217 79.2,但 GPQA 71.5 对 75.7、SimpleQA 30.1 对 47.0 仍落后。Gudibande 等(ICLR 2024;2023 年、1.5B–13B 基座)发现,模仿模型在模仿数据未充分覆盖的任务上,几乎没有缩小基座与 ChatGPT 的差距。Anthropic 2026-02-23 单方指控 DeepSeek、Moonshot、MiniMax 通过约 24,000 个欺诈账户与 Claude 产生 1600 万次以上交互(其中 MiniMax 约 1300 万),并称非法蒸馏可以用一小部分时间和成本获得能力;三家截至目前未见正式公开回应。MEM1 附录消融显示,在 GPT-4o 轨迹上 SFT 的模型,目标数超过 6 个时性能崩溃,而 RL 模型保持稳健。

**口径修正**:
- (a) 属实;o1-1217 对比数为 DeepSeek 自报,标“厂商口径”;72.6 vs 47.0 的对比属同一基座(Qwen-32B)上蒸馏与 RL 的对照
- (b) 属实,ICLR 2024 确认;须补限定“2023 年、1.5B–13B 基座、ChatGPT 模仿数据”
- (c) 数字逐字属实;“fraction of the time/cost”须写成 Anthropic 对非法蒸馏的一般性说法,而非实测;三方未见正式回应——写“截至目前未见公开回应”,并标为 Anthropic 单方指控
- (d) 逐字属实;补限定“附录消融,SFT 用 GPT-4o 轨迹在 ≤3 目标上训练”

**核对过程**:(a) R1 v1 PDF:表(行 730–731)DeepSeek-R1-Zero-Qwen-32B AIME24 47.0、R1-Distill-Qwen-32B 72.6;行 742–743 逐字 'while distillation strategies are both economical and effective, advancing beyond the boundaries of intelligence may still require more powerful base models and larger-scale reinforcement learning'。主表:AIME 2024 o1-1217 79.2 / R1 79.8;GPQA Diamond 75.7 / 71.5;SimpleQA 47.0 / 30.1,全部吻合。o1 数字是 DeepSeek 自报表中的数值,非独立复测。(b) 2305.15717 摘要逐字 'imitation models close little to none of the gap from the base LM to ChatGPT on tasks that are not heavily supported in the imitation data'。已发表于 ICLR 2024(poster 18877;会议版标题为 '...Proprietary Language Models')。规模限定:基座 1.5B–13B、2023 年 ChatGPT 模仿数据,不能直接外推到 R1 式大规模推理蒸馏(两者结论在表面上有张力,写作时须交代)。(c) Anthropic 2026-02-23 原文逐字:'These labs generated over 16 million exchanges with Claude through approximately 24,000 fraudulent accounts';'in a fraction of the time, and at a fraction of the cost'——这句是对非法蒸馏的一般性描述,不是针对这三家实验室的实测结论。分实验室:DeepSeek 15 万以上、Moonshot 340 万以上、MiniMax 1300 万以上,DeepSeek 占比很小。回应:检索未发现三家正式公开回应或否认(媒体报道称“未回应置评请求”);Musk 等第三方有批评。另有 2026-09 后续报告的二手报道称数字更大,未核。(d) MEM1 附录 D.1 逐字 'when the number of objectives exceeds six, the performance of the SFT model collapses, whereas the RL-trained model continues to demonstrate strong robustness'。限定:SFT 模型用 GPT-4o 整理的轨迹在 1–3 目标 QA 上训练,是附录里的单一消融。

来源:https://arxiv.org/pdf/2501.12948v1 (pdftotext 抽取);https://arxiv.org/pdf/2305.15717v1;https://iclr.cc/virtual/2024/poster/18877(检索确认);https://www.anthropic.com/news/detecting-and-preventing-distillation-attacks (curl 原文);https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.15841

# G24 — 票型 ['CORRECTED', 'CORRECTED', 'CORRECTED']

## 票 1:CORRECTED(单源已核)

**修正后表述**:Compute ownership is concentrated. Epoch (2026-04-14) estimates that Amazon, Google, Meta, Microsoft and Oracle owned about 71% of global cumulative AI compute in Q4 2025, up from 63% in Q1 2024 (ownership, not usage). The record for the largest single data center has doubled about every 7 months since August 2024. Epoch's data center dataset (2026-10-12) holds about 14.6M H100e, 86% of it in the US. Six of the seven sites at or above 500k H100e are in the US, but the dataset covers only about 44% of global compute (90% CI 23–81%). On training compute, GPT-6 Astra is about 1e27 FLOP (CI 5e26–2e27). The largest open-weights model released in 2026, Kimi K3 (non-commercial licence), is a speculative estimate of about 2e25, roughly 50x less. DeepSeek-V4-Pro is about 100x less. In a December 2025 counterfactual with no exports and no smuggling, IFP estimated a US advantage of 21–49x in 2026-produced AI compute. Epoch estimates Huawei will produce less than 4% of Nvidia's compute output in 2026. Lin Junyang said US compute may exceed China's by one to two orders of magnitude, and put the chance that the world's leading AI company is Chinese in 3–5 years at 20%, adding that this was already very optimistic.

**口径修正**:
- (a) HOLDS verbatim. Add the qualifier: this is chip ownership, measured in H100e, of cumulative compute. It is not usage share, and AI labs mostly rent their compute.
- (b) HOLDS. Add 'since August 2024 (Colossus 1)'. Epoch itself forecasts the record will stay roughly flat from Colossus 2 until H2 2027.
- (c) 14.6M H100e, 86.3% US, and 6 of 7 sites ≥500k in the US all reproduce. 'Serves OpenAI/Anthropic/Google/Meta/xAI' needs two corrections: Colossus 2's listed users are now Anthropic, Cursor and SpaceXAI (xAI is now SpaceXAI), and Google Pryor's DeepMind user is tagged #speculative. Also, 86.3% is a share of the dataset, not of world compute.
- Ruled out: 'the dataset covers only about 26% of global compute'. The page says 44% as of 2026-10-13 (90% CI 23–81%), and Q1 2024 was 22%.
- (d) Ruled out: 'the largest open model is Kimi K3'. In the dataset, Llama Nemotron Ultra 253B (3.9e25) and Llama 3.1-405B (3.8e25) are both larger. Kimi K3 is also open weights under a non-commercial licence. Corrected wording: 'Among open-weights models released in 2026, the largest is Kimi K3 at about 2e25 (speculative), about 50x below GPT-6 Astra's 1e27 (CI 5e26–2e27). DeepSeek-V4-Pro (9.7e24) is about 100x below.'
- (e) Verbatim HOLDS. You must keep 'Dec 2025, counterfactual baseline with no exports and no smuggling, range driven by FP4 vs FP8'. Do not write it as a forecast of actual US–China compute.
- (f) Verbatim HOLDS (report Sep 4, revised Sep 24, conclusion unchanged). Make clear this is Huawei vs Nvidia only.
- (g) Verbatim HOLDS (ChinaTalk English translation). The 20% answers 'the probability that the leading AI company will be a Chinese one in 3–5 years', and Lin himself added that 20% 'is already very optimistic'.

**核对过程**:(a) Epoch data insight, dated Apr. 14, 2026, by Emberson/You/Somala. Exact wording: 'Amazon, Google, Meta, Microsoft, and Oracle collectively hold an estimated 71% of the world's cumulative AI compute as of Q4 2025 ... up from 63% in Q1 2024'. The table shows 62.5% for Q1 2024. This counts chip ownership, not usage. (b) The Data Insight dated Jun. 11, 2026 (Ben Cottier) says: 'Since the launch of SpaceXAI's Colossus 1 in August 2024, the record for the largest AI data center by computing capacity has doubled every seven months.' Its own forecast table shows Colossus 2 at 1.1e6 in 2026-07, with the next jump not until 2027-10 (2.0e6), so the trend is expected to pause. (c) I downloaded data_centers.csv on 2026-10-12. It has 94 rows. Summing 'Current H100 equivalents' gives 14,619,636. The US share is 86.34%. Seven sites are at or above 500k H100e: Colossus 2 (1.11M, US; users Anthropic, Cursor, SpaceXAI), Microsoft Fairwater Atlanta (769k, US; OpenAI #likely), Anthropic-Amazon New Carlisle (686k, US), Google Pryor North (637k, US; user Google DeepMind tagged #speculative), DayOne Nusajaya (584k, Malaysia, no owner or user listed), Meta Prometheus (536k, US) and OpenAI Stargate Abilene (509k, US). So 6 of 7 in the US is correct. The coverage figure is wrong. The live dataset page says: 'we estimate this database's coverage of global deployed AI computing capacity to be 44% as of October 13, 2026 (90% CI: 23% to 81%)'. It also says coverage was 22% in Q1 2024. 26% is not the current figure. (d) all_ai_models.csv: GPT-6 Astra is 1.0001e27 (Confidence Likely), and its notes say 'CI of ~[5e26, 2e27] FLOP'. Kimi K3 is 2.0001e25 (Speculative), with accessibility 'Open weights (non-commercial)'. DeepSeek-V4-Pro is 9.702e24 (Likely, unrestricted open weights). Kimi K3 is not the largest open-weights model in the dataset. Sorting all open-weights models by training compute gives Llama Nemotron Ultra 253B at 3.91e25 (restricted use, 2025-03) and Llama 3.1-405B at 3.8e25, both above K3. So 'largest open model = K3' only holds if limited to 2026 releases or to Chinese models. 1e27/2e25 = 50x, and 1e27/9.7e24 ≈ 103x. Both are pretraining FLOP estimates, and K3's is speculative. (e) The IFP PDF dated Dec 6/7, 2025 contains the exact sentence: 'With no AI chip exports to China and no smuggling, we estimate the US would hold a 21–49x advantage in 2026-produced AI compute, depending on whether FP4 or FP8 performance is used for Blackwell chips.' This is a counterfactual baseline written before the H200 export decision. The same report says unrestricted H200 exports would shrink the advantage to 6.7x–1.2x. (f) Epoch report 'Will Huawei catch up to Nvidia by 2030?' by Venkat Somala, dated Sep. 4, 2026 and updated Sep. 24, 2026. The update says revisions 'do not affect the report's main conclusions'. Exact wording: 'Huawei will produce less than 4% of Nvidia's compute output this year.' The report gives a 6% upper extreme of its CI, about 1% through 2028 with only Chinese HBM, and 1.4% when measured by memory bandwidth. The comparison is Huawei vs Nvidia, not China vs the US. (g) The ChinaTalk transcript has, verbatim, Lin Junyang (Qwen): 'U.S. compute may overall exceed ours by one to two orders of magnitude.' The moderator's question was 'Three to five years from now, what's the probability that the leading AI company will be a Chinese one?' Lin answered: 'I think it's 20%. Twenty percent is already very optimistic'.

来源:https://epoch.ai/data-insights/hyperscalers-control-most-compute ; https://epoch.ai/data-insights/largest-data-center-compute ; https://epoch.ai/data/data-centers (page text, fetched 2026-10-12) ; https://epoch.ai/data/data_centers/data_centers.csv ; https://epoch.ai/data/all_ai_models.csv ; https://ifp.org/wp-content/uploads/Should-the-US-Sell-Hopper-Chips-to-China-IFP.pdf ; https://epoch.ai/publications/huaweis-roadmap-to-2031 ; https://www.chinatalk.media/p/the-all-star-chinese-ai-conversation

## 票 2:CORRECTED(单源已核)

**修正后表述**:Epoch estimates the five hyperscalers held about 71% of global cumulative AI compute in Q4 2025, up from 63% in Q1 2024 (unfinalized Q1 2026 data is 75.8%). Since August 2024 the record for the largest single data center has doubled about every 7 months, though Epoch expects it to stall at Colossus 2 until H2 2027. In Epoch's data-center dataset (94 sites, about 14.6M H100e, which Epoch says covers about 44% of global deployed compute) the US holds 86%, and 6 of the 7 sites at or above 500k H100e are in the US. GPT-6 Astra used about 1e27 FLOP (CI 5e26–2e27), roughly 50x the largest 2026 Chinese open-weight model Kimi K3 (~2e25, speculative) and roughly 26x the largest open-weight model ever (Llama 3.1-405B lineage, ~3.9e25). IFP estimated that with no exports and no smuggling the US would hold a 21–49x advantage in 2026-produced compute, falling to 6.7–1.2x under unrestricted H200 exports. Epoch forecasts Huawei will produce less than 4% of Nvidia's AI compute in 2026. Lin Junyang said US compute may exceed China's by 'one to two orders of magnitude' and put the chance that a Chinese company leads in 3–5 years at 20%, adding that this was 'already very optimistic'.

**口径修正**:
- (a) Holds. Can add that Epoch's table shows Q1 2026 at 75.8% (not finalized); the data source does not cover all global ownership
- (b) 'record doubles about every 7 months' → 'since Colossus 1 in Aug 2024 the record has doubled about every 7 months, and Epoch expects it to plateau at Colossus 2 until H2 2027'
- (c) Kill: 'dataset covers only about 26% of the world' → Epoch's own estimate is 44% (as of 2026-10-13, 94 sites). Everything else in (c) recomputes correctly; mark Pryor/Google as speculative and Fairwater/OpenAI as likely
- (d) 'largest open model Kimi K3 ~2e25 → about 50x' → 'GPT-6 Astra ~1e27 (CI 5e26–2e27) is about 50x the largest 2026 Chinese open-weight model, Kimi K3 (~2e25, speculative, non-commercial licence)'. Against all open-weight models the largest is Llama Nemotron Ultra / Llama 3.1-405B (~3.9e25), about 26x
- (e) Must add: IFP 2025-12-07 counterfactual; under unrestricted H200 exports the same report gives 6.7x–1.2x
- (f) Quote corrected to 'less than 4% of Nvidia's AI compute in 2026' (Epoch X post, forecast)
- (g) Holds; can add 'Twenty percent is already very optimistic'

**核对过程**:(a) The Epoch page says verbatim: 'Amazon, Google, Meta, Microsoft, and Oracle collectively hold an estimated 71%', 'as of Q4 2025', 'up from 63% in Q1 2024'. Its caveats: chip-ownership data does not capture all global ownership, and Q1 2026 was excluded as not finalized. The page's own table already shows 75.8% for Q1 2026, so concentration kept rising (counter-direction check passed). (b) The Epoch data insight (2026-06-11) says verbatim that the record 'has doubled every seven months' 'Since the launch of SpaceXAI's Colossus 1 in August 2024'. The same page predicts 'no data center will have meaningfully greater capacity than Colossus 2 until the second half of 2027'. This is a short-window trend plus a stated plateau. Do not confuse it with Epoch's other figure, global chip capacity 3.3x/year (also about a 7-month doubling). (c) Python recomputation on data_centers.csv: 94 sites, total 14,619,636 H100e, US share 86.34%. Sites at or above 500k H100e: Colossus 2 1.11M, Fairwater Atlanta 0.77M, Anthropic-Amazon New Carlisle 0.69M, Google Pryor 0.64M, DayOne Nusajaya (Malaysia) 0.58M, Meta Prometheus 0.54M, Stargate Abilene 0.51M. That is 7 sites, 6 in the US, so this holds. Two notes: Colossus 2's owner is now listed as 'SpaceXAI' and its users include Anthropic and Cursor; the Pryor→Google user tag is #speculative and Fairwater→OpenAI is #likely. The '~26% coverage' figure is contradicted: the Epoch Frontier Data Centers page says verbatim 'we estimate this database's coverage of global deployed AI computing capacity to be 44% as of October 13, 2026' (94 data centers). (d) all_ai_models.csv: GPT-6 Astra is 1.0001e27, confidence Likely, notes 'CI of ~[5e26, 2e27]'. Kimi K3 is 2.0e25, Speculative, licence 'Open weights (non-commercial)'. DeepSeek-V4-Pro is 9.70e24, Likely. But sorting by training compute, the largest open-weight models are Llama Nemotron Ultra 253B (3.9e25, restricted) and Llama 3.1-405B (3.8e25, Confident), followed by Kimi K3. 'Largest open model = Kimi K3' only holds for 2026 releases or Chinese models. Against all open weights the gap is about 26x, not 50x. (e) The IFP page (2025-12-07) contains verbatim 'we estimate the US would hold a 21–49x advantage in 2026-produced AI compute', with the conditions no exports and no smuggling, and FP4 vs FP8 explaining the range. The same report says 'Unrestricted H200 exports would shrink this advantage to between 6.7x and 1.2x'. The US approved H200 sales in Dec 2025, so the 21–49x is a counterfactual baseline, not a realized figure. (f) The Epoch X post (2026-09-04) reads verbatim 'Huawei will produce less than 4% of Nvidia's AI compute in 2026'. The quote in the claim, 'compute output this year', is not the original wording. It is a forecast, and the full report was not found. (g) ChinaTalk translation verbatim: 'U.S. compute may overall exceed ours by one to two orders of magnitude'. On the probability that the leading company 3–5 years out is Chinese, he said 'I think it's 20%' and added 'Twenty percent is already very optimistic'. On the same stage Yao Shunyu was optimistic, a counter-view.

来源:https://epoch.ai/data-insights/hyperscalers-control-most-compute ; https://epoch.ai/data-insights/largest-data-center-compute ; https://epoch.ai/data/data_centers/data_centers.csv (downloaded 2026-10-12) ; https://epoch.ai/data/frontier-data-centers ; https://epoch.ai/data/all_ai_models.csv (downloaded) ; https://ifp.org/should-the-us-sell-hopper-chips-to-china/ ; Epoch X post 2026-09-04 via https://ec.ltn.com.tw/article/breakingnews/5564221 ; https://www.chinatalk.media/p/the-all-star-chinese-ai-conversation

## 票 3:CORRECTED(单源已核)

**修正后表述**:Inputs are concentrated. Epoch estimates that five hyperscalers (Amazon, Google, Meta, Microsoft, Oracle) owned about 71% of global cumulative AI compute in Q4 2025, up from 63% in Q1 2024. The compute record for a single data center has doubled roughly every 7 months. Epoch's data-center database (snapshot 2026-10-12) holds about 14.6M H100e, 86% of it in the US, and 6 of the 7 sites above 500K H100e are in the US, serving xAI/SpaceXAI, Anthropic, OpenAI, Google and Meta. The database is built US-first and covers an estimated 44% of global capacity (90% CI 23–81%). Epoch estimates GPT-6 Astra at about 1e27 FLOP (CI 5e26–2e27). The largest open-weight model of 2026, Kimi K3, is about 2e25 (speculative), roughly 50x apart (25–100x across the CIs); the largest open-weight model ever, Llama 3.1-405B, is about 3.8e25, roughly 26x. In December 2025 IFP estimated that, with no exports and no smuggling, the US would hold a 21–49x advantage in AI compute produced in 2026. Epoch estimates Huawei's 2026 AI compute output at under 4% of Nvidia's. Lin Junyang said U.S. compute 'may overall exceed ours by one to two orders of magnitude', and put the chance that the leading AI company in 3–5 years is Chinese at 20%, calling that 'already very optimistic'.

**口径修正**:
- (a) HOLDS. Add the qualifiers 'ownership (not usage), cumulative H100e', and name Oracle among the five
- (b) HOLDS (Epoch Data Insight, 2026-06-11)
- (c) Proven wrong: 'the dataset covers only about 26% of the world' → Epoch's own estimate is coverage of about 44% as of 2026-10-13 (90% CI 23%–81%); Q1 2024 is 22%. 14.6M / 86.3% / 7 sites with 6 in the US all recompute correctly. Add: the database is built US-first, so the 86.3% is the share within this sample, not the global US share
- (d) Proven wrong: 'the largest open-weight model is Kimi K3' → the largest open-weight model is Llama 3.1-405B (3.8e25, 2024). Kimi K3 (2e25, speculative, non-commercial license) is the largest open-weight / Chinese model of 2026. Change '~50x' to 'about 50x vs. Kimi K3 (25–100x across the CIs), about 26x vs. the largest open-weight model ever', and note that GPT-6 is 'Likely' and K3 is 'Speculative'
- (e) Add the date and scenario: IFP December 2025, a counterfactual baseline with no exports and no smuggling; the same report says unrestricted H200 exports would shrink it to 6.7x–1.2x
- (f) HOLDS
- (g) HOLDS. The 20% answers 'probability that the leading AI company in 3–5 years is Chinese', and he added 'already very optimistic'. His affiliation was Alibaba Qwen at the time of the talk

**核对过程**:(a) The Epoch data insight's metadata reads word for word: 'Amazon, Google, Meta, Microsoft, and Oracle collectively hold an estimated 71% of the world's cumulative AI compute as of Q4 2025, up from 63% in Q1 2024'. The metric is ownership in H100e, not usage. (b) The Epoch data-center page lists the Data Insight of Jun 11, 2026 titled 'The record for computing capacity in a single data center has doubled every 7 months'. (c) I recomputed from data_centers.csv: 94 sites, sum of 'Current H100 equivalents' = 14.62M, US share = 86.34%. Sites at or above 500K H100e: Colossus 2 (1.11M, US, SpaceXAI/Anthropic), Fairwater Atlanta (769K, US, OpenAI/MSFT), Anthropic-Amazon New Carlisle (686K, US), Google Pryor (637K, US), DayOne Nusajaya (584K, Malaysia, users blank), Meta Prometheus (536K, US), Stargate Abilene (509K, US, OpenAI). That is 7 sites, 6 in the US, so the claim checks out. The coverage figure does not. The page reads verbatim: 'we estimate this database's coverage of global deployed AI computing capacity to be 44% as of October 13, 2026 (90% CI: 23% to 81%)', with Q1 2024 at 22%. 26% does not appear anywhere on the page. The FAQ also says coverage is US-first ('most of the largest data centers are in the US'), so the 86.3% is partly a sampling artifact. (d) all_ai_models.csv: GPT-6 Astra = 1e27 (Confidence Likely); the notes say 'CI of ~[5e26, 2e27]'. Kimi K3 = 2.0e25 (Speculative, Open weights non-commercial). DeepSeek-V4-Pro = 9.7e24 (pretraining only, 6ND). However, the largest open-weight model in the dataset is not Kimi K3. It is Llama 3.1-405B at 3.8e25 (Confident, 2024), and Llama Nemotron Ultra 253B carries 3.9e25 because it includes its base model's compute. 1e27/2e25 = 50x, but against the largest open model 1e27/3.8e25 ≈ 26x, and against DeepSeek-V4-Pro it is about 100x. Folding in the GPT-6 CI, the gap to K3 is 25–100x. (e) The IFP PDF (Dec 6/7, 2025) reads word for word: 'With no AI chip exports to China and no smuggling, we estimate the US would hold a 21–49x advantage in 2026-produced AI compute, depending on whether FP4 or FP8 performance is used for Blackwell chips'. In the same passage, unrestricted H200 exports shrink this to 6.7x–1.2x. This is a counterfactual baseline, and US policy later moved toward allowing H200 exports. (f) The Epoch 'Will Huawei catch up to Nvidia by 2030?' page reads: 'Huawei will produce less than 4% as much AI compute as Nvidia in 2026, a share that could be around 1% by 2028 without access to foreign memory'. (g) The ChinaTalk transcript reads word for word: 'U.S. compute may overall exceed ours by one to two orders of magnitude'. On the probability question, the moderator asked whether the leading AI company in 3–5 years 'will be a Chinese one'. Lin answered 'I think it's 20%. Twenty percent is already very optimistic'.

来源:https://epoch.ai/data-insights/hyperscalers-control-most-compute ; https://epoch.ai/data/data-centers (page text + data_centers.csv downloaded 2026-10-12) ; https://epoch.ai/data/all_ai_models.csv ; https://ifp.org/wp-content/uploads/Should-the-US-Sell-Hopper-Chips-to-China-IFP.pdf ; https://epoch.ai/publications/huaweis-roadmap-to-2031 ; https://www.chinatalk.media/p/the-all-star-chinese-ai-conversation

# G25 — 票型 ['CORRECTED', 'CORRECTED', 'CORRECTED']

## 票 1:CORRECTED(单源已核)

**修正后表述**:By my own calculation on Epoch ECI data downloaded 2026-10-12, the frontier record has risen about 15.6 ECI per year since 2024, so 10 ECI is about 7.7 months. If one roughly assumes frontier compute grows 5x per year and progress is driven by compute, that equals about 2.8x compute, far smaller than the roughly 50–100x gap in pretraining FLOP between frontier and open-weights models (an estimate). Meta's best, Muse Spark 1.3 (156.75), and xAI's Grok 4.6 (156.44) are about 10.6–10.9 below the frontier (167.3), in the same tier as Kimi K3 (157.45). Using a 6-ECI threshold, five organizations were close to the frontier in September 2025, but only Anthropic and OpenAI were by June and September 2026; Google's latest Gemini 4 Argon has not been scored. Ho et al. (2024) estimate that the compute needed to reach a given performance threshold halved about every 8 months (95% CI about 5–14 months). Gundlach et al. find that small-scale ablations account for less than 10x of the 22,000x, and that two scale-dependent innovations account for 91% of gains extrapolated to the 2025 frontier, concluding that measures of algorithmic efficiency are 'strongly reference-dependent'. Epoch estimates OpenAI spent about $5B on R&D compute in 2024, with final training runs only about 9.6% of that. It suggests that if most of the spend is exploration, a follower could replicate for a fraction of the cost, but says three data points are inconclusive.

**口径修正**:
- (a) The slope reproduces at 15.59 per year. Converting it into '2.8x compute' is the author's own assumption (5x per year growth, with all ECI progress attributed to compute) and must be labelled as a rough estimate. The 50x is a speculative pretraining-FLOP estimate for K3; against DeepSeek-V4-Pro it is about 100x. Note that ECI is refitted with every data update.
- (b) HOLDS, but the precise gaps are 10.6 (Meta) and 10.9 (xAI); write 'about 10.5–11'. Kimi K3, Gemini 3.7 Flash and Qwen 3.8 Max are in the same tier.
- (c) Reproduces (5 → 2 → 2), with caveats: the result depends on the 6-ECI threshold (Qwen was at 6.15 in 2025-09) and on the current ECI version. Gemini 4 Argon is limited access and has no score.
- (d) Verbatim HOLDS. Add 'language models, 2012–2023'.
- (e) Verbatim HOLDS. The 91% means two scale-dependent innovations (LSTM→Transformer and Chinchilla rebalancing) account for 91% of total efficiency gains extrapolated to the 2025 compute frontier. The 22,000x is Ho et al.'s figure, cited by this paper, not its own.
- (f) The figures are verbatim. 'A competitor could replicate for a fraction of the cost' is an 'if…then' conditional, and the authors call the three-data-point evidence inconclusive (Z.ai does not support catch-up). It cannot be written as an established conclusion.

**核对过程**:(a) Recomputation: I sorted eci_scores.csv by date and built the series of ECI frontier records (each point that sets a new all-time maximum). Taking the 21 record points from 2024-01-01 onward (GPT-4 Turbo 126.46 through Claude Opus 5.5 167.33 on 2026-09-22) and fitting OLS of ECI on years gives a slope of 15.59 per year. The endpoint method gives about 15.4 per year. 10/15.59 = 0.641 years = 7.7 months, and 5^0.641 = 2.81x. ln50/ln5 = 2.43 years. The arithmetic reproduces. However, '10 ECI ≈ 2.8x frontier compute' assumes all ECI progress comes from growth in training compute and that compute grows 5x per year. That is the author's mapping, not an Epoch finding. The 50x also uses Kimi K3's speculative pretraining estimate; against DeepSeek-V4-Pro it would be about 100x (about 2.9 years). (b) In eci_scores.csv, Meta AI's best model is Muse Spark 1.3 at 156.75 (CI 154.78–159.34) and xAI's best is Grok 4.6 at 156.44. The frontier is Claude Opus 5.5 at 167.33. The gaps are 10.58 and 10.89. Kimi K3 is 157.45. Google's best scored model, Gemini 3.7 Flash, is 157.27, and Alibaba Qwen 3.8 Max is 156.41, so they sit in the same tier. (c) For each date T, I took the frontier as the maximum ECI among models dated ≤T, and for each organization its best model dated ≤T. At 2025-09-30 (frontier GPT-5, 150.0), five organizations were within 6: OpenAI, Anthropic 3.16, xAI 3.56, Google 4.68 and DeepSeek 5.0. Qwen was at 6.15, just outside. At 2026-06-30 (frontier 162.07) there were two: OpenAI and Anthropic 0.01. Google was 7.3 behind. At 2026-09-30 there were two: Anthropic and OpenAI 0.88. Gemini 4 Argon appears in all_ai_models.csv (2026-09-30, Limited access) but not in eci_scores.csv. The result reproduces, but it depends on the 6-point threshold (2025-09 Qwen sits on the edge) and on the current ECI refit. (d) The 2403.05812 abstract reads verbatim: 'the compute required to reach a set performance threshold has halved approximately every 8 months, with a 95% confidence interval of around 5 to 14 months'. This is for pretraining language models, 2012–2023. (e) The 2511.21622 abstract reads verbatim: 'Algorithms have been estimated to increase AI training FLOP efficiency by a factor of 22,000 between 2012 and 2023 [Ho et al., 2024]... we are able to account for less than 10× of these gains' and '...measures of algorithmic efficiency are strongly reference-dependent.' On the 91%, the paper says the two scale-dependent innovations, LSTM→Transformer and Kaplan→Chinchilla, 'account for 91% of total efficiency gains when extrapolating to the 2025 compute frontier'. In the body this is 91% of the measured 21,400x, with 846x and about 10x respectively. (f) Epoch Gradient Updates, dated Mar. 23, 2026 (Denain & Wu). Verbatim: 'OpenAI spent $5 billion on R&D compute in 2024'; 'final training runs account for... 9.6% for OpenAI, 22.6% for MiniMax, and 12.3% for Z.ai'. The quoted phrase appears in a conditional sentence: 'if most of the spending is exploration rather than execution, then a competitor who learns what works from the frontier could replicate the results for a fraction of the original cost.' The authors also say 'with only three data points, the evidence remains inconclusive', and that Z.ai does not fit the catch-up prediction.

来源:https://epoch.ai/data/benchmark_data.zip (epoch_capabilities_index/eci_scores.csv, downloaded 2026-10-12) ; https://epoch.ai/data/all_ai_models.csv ; arXiv PDF 2403.05812 ; arXiv PDF 2511.21622 ; https://epoch.ai/gradient-updates/r-and-d-vs-training-compute

## 票 2:CORRECTED(单源已核)

**修正后表述**:On Epoch's 2026-10 ECI data, the frontier record has risen about 15.6 points per year since 2024 (about 20.8 if counted from 2023). By that pace, 10 ECI ≈ 7.7 months. If all of it is attributed to frontier compute growth of 5x per year, that is about 2.8x compute, well below the roughly 2.4 years implied by the ~50x training-compute gap between GPT-6 Astra and Kimi K3 (Kimi's compute is a speculative estimate). Meta's Muse Spark 1.3 (156.75) and xAI's Grok 4.6 (156.44) are about 10.5 points below the frontier of 167.3, in the same tier as Kimi K3 (157.45) and Gemini 3.7 Flash (157.27). Under today's ECI refit, five labs had a best model within 6 points of the frontier in September 2025, but only Anthropic and OpenAI did in June and September 2026. This is a retrospective measure, and Gemini 4 Argon (announced 9-30, limited access) has not been scored. Ho et al. found that 2012–2023 pretraining compute needed to reach a set performance threshold halved about every 8 months (95% CI 5–14 months). Gundlach et al. found that small-scale ablations explain less than 10x of the 22,000x, and that two scale-dependent innovations account for 91% of measured gains when extrapolated to the 2025 frontier, concluding that 'measures of algorithmic efficiency are strongly reference-dependent'. Epoch estimates that final training runs were only 9.6% of OpenAI's roughly $5B 2024 R&D compute (12.3% at Z.ai, 22.6% at MiniMax), and infers that a follower could 'replicate the results for a fraction of the original cost'.

**口径修正**:
- (a) Recomputation holds (15.6/year, 7.7 months, 2.8x, 2.4 years). Must add: the slope is start-date sensitive (20.8 from 2023); the 50x compares GPT-6 Astra with Kimi K3 (speculative compute), not the ECI frontier model; converting ECI to a compute multiple assumes all progress comes from compute
- (b) Holds; can add that Google's Gemini 3.7 Flash (157.27) is in the same tier
- (c) Recomputation holds. Must mark it as a retrospective measure under the 2026-10 ECI refit with a 6-point threshold; at 8 points June 2026 has 3 labs; Gemini 4 Argon was announced 9-30 with limited access and is unscored, so the conclusion is provisional
- (d) Holds; add the qualifier 'pretraining language modeling on WikiText/PTB, 2012–2023'
- (e) '91% from scale-dependent innovations' → 'two scale-dependent innovations (LSTM→Transformer, Kaplan→Chinchilla) account for 91% of measured efficiency gains when extrapolated to the 2025 compute frontier'
- (f) Holds; add that $5B is a reported figure and the replication phrase is conditional (n=3)

**核对过程**:(a) In eci_scores.csv, an OLS fit on the record-setting points since 2024-01-01 gives 15.59 points/year. Fitting the monthly running maximum instead gives 15.71 since 2024 and 14.72 since 2025, so the result is fairly robust. Starting in 2023 gives 20.8, so the start date matters. 10/15.59 = 0.64 years = 7.7 months, and 5^0.64 ≈ 2.8x. log5(50) = 2.43 years. The arithmetic reproduces. However, the 50x compute gap pairs GPT-6 Astra (whose ECI 166.45 is not the frontier) with Kimi K3, whose compute is speculative. Training compute for the frontier model Claude Opus 5.5 is unknown. And 'ECI per year → compute multiple' implicitly attributes all progress to compute growth, ignoring algorithmic and post-training contributions. (b) Muse Spark 1.3 156.75 and Grok 4.6 156.44 sit 10.58 and 10.89 below the frontier of 167.33. Kimi K3 is 157.45 (9.88 below). Google Gemini 3.7 Flash, at 157.27, is also in this band. Holds. (c) For each date, frontier = maximum ECI released up to that date, and each lab's best model up to that date. At 2025-09-30 five labs were within 6 points: OpenAI 0, Anthropic 3.2, xAI 3.6, Google 4.7, DeepSeek 5.0. At 2026-06-30 only OpenAI and Anthropic were (Google 7.3). At 2026-09-30 only Anthropic and OpenAI (0.9) were. Holds. Caveats: these are retrospective scores from the latest ECI refit, not what was observed at the time, and the 6-point threshold is arbitrary (at 8 points, June 2026 has 3 labs). There is no Gemini 4 or Argon in the data. Gemini 4 Argon was announced 2026-09-30 for limited access only (cyber defenders); Google self-reports it as comparable to Astra and Opus, so the 'only 2 labs' conclusion may be overturned shortly. (d) The 2403.05812 abstract is verbatim correct, but the data cover WikiText/PTB pretraining perplexity for 2012–2023, so the qualifier needs keeping. (e) The 2511.21622 abstract has '22,000', 'less than 10x', and 'measures of algorithmic efficiency are strongly reference-dependent', all verbatim. The 91% comes from the body text: 'Together, these account for 91% of total efficiency gains when extrapolating to the 2025 compute frontier'. It refers to two specific scale-dependent innovations (LSTM→Transformer and Kaplan→Chinchilla), extrapolated to 2e23 FLOP and measured against 21,400x. It is not the share of all scale-dependent innovations, and the paper accounts for 6,930x in total. (f) Epoch Gradient Update 2026-03-23 (Denain and Wu): OpenAI 9.6%, MiniMax 22.6%, Z.ai 12.3%, three data points. The phrase 'could replicate the results for a fraction of the original cost' is verbatim but sits inside a conditional. The $5B is a reported figure (Epoch's 2025-10-10 data insight), not an Epoch measurement.

来源:https://epoch.ai/data/benchmark_data.zip (eci_scores.csv, downloaded 2026-10-12, recomputed in Python) ; arXiv 2403.05812 abstract ; arXiv 2511.21622 full-text PDF ; https://epoch.ai/gradient-updates/r-and-d-vs-training-compute ; https://epoch.ai/data-insights/openai-compute-spend ; Gemini 4 Argon news (khaleejtimes/gizmodo.jp)

## 票 3:CORRECTED(单源已核)

**修正后表述**:Own recomputation (Epoch ECI, data as of 2026-10-12): a linear fit to frontier ECI records since 2024 gives about 15.6 points per year, so 10 ECI ≈ 7.7 months. Assuming frontier compute grows 5x a year, that is roughly 2.8x compute, far smaller than the roughly 50x training compute gap between GPT-6 Astra and Kimi K3 (25–100x across the CIs, about 2.4 years at 5x/yr). Meta's Muse Spark 1.3 (156.75) and xAI's Grok 4.6 (156.44) trail the frontier, Claude Opus 5.5 at 167.33, by about 10.6 and 10.9 points, the same tier as Kimi K3 (157.45) and Gemini 3.7 Flash (157.27). Counting only ECI-scored models, 5 labs had a best model within 6 ECI of the frontier in September 2025, and only 2 (Anthropic, OpenAI) in June and September 2026. Google's Gemini 4 Argon has not been scored. Ho et al. find that the compute needed to reach a given language-modeling performance halves about every 8 months (95% CI around 5–14 months). Gundlach et al. find that small-scale ablations explain less than 10x of the 22,000x, and that the scale-dependent shifts (LSTM→Transformer, Chinchilla) account for 91% of measured progress, so 'measures of algorithmic efficiency are strongly reference-dependent'. Epoch estimates OpenAI's 2024 R&D compute at about $5B, with final training about 9.6% of it, and argues that if most spending is exploration, 'a competitor who learns what works from the frontier could replicate the results for a fraction of the original cost'. Epoch adds that with only three data points the evidence is inconclusive.

**口径修正**:
- (a) The recomputation checks out at 15.59/yr. Label it 'own calculation, fitted on ECI frontier records, assuming 5x/yr frontier compute growth, current ECI version'. If you keep the 50x, note it is against Kimi K3 with a 25–100x CI, which gives a lag of about 2.0–2.9 years
- (b) 'both about 10.5 lower' → Muse Spark 1.3 is 10.6 lower and Grok 4.6 is 10.9 lower. Google's best scored model, Gemini 3.7 Flash (157.27), is in the same tier
- (c) The counts recompute correctly, but qualify: 'among ECI-scored models, at a 6-point threshold'. Google's Gemini 4 Argon (2026-09-30, limited release, reportedly near the frontier on Arena/AA) is unscored, so the '2 labs' finding may be overturned when it is scored
- (d) HOLDS. Add the scope: pretraining language modeling (perplexity), 2012–2023
- (e) HOLDS. The 91% is the share within the measured 21,400x attributed to LSTM→Transformer plus Chinchilla rebalancing (scale-dependent)
- (f) Keep the 'if most spending is exploration' condition from the quoted original. Mark it as Gradient Updates (commentary) and note that Epoch itself says three data points are 'inconclusive'. The $5B is Epoch's estimate for OpenAI's 2024 R&D compute, 9.6% of which went to final training

**核对过程**:(a) I recomputed the ECI frontier record staircase. Each new all-time high counts as a record: GPT-4 Turbo 2024-01-25 at 126.46 through Claude Opus 5.5 2026-09-22 at 167.33, 21 points from 2024 onward. OLS gives 15.59 ECI/yr. 10/15.59 yr = 0.641 yr = 7.7 months, 5^0.641 = 2.81x, and log5(50) = 2.43 yr all check out. Method choices to disclose: the fit uses only record points, '5x/yr' is an assumed growth rate for frontier compute, and equating ECI with calendar time with compute folds algorithmic and post-training progress into compute. ECI is refit with every data update, so all values belong to this one snapshot. (b) Meta Muse Spark 1.3 = 156.75 (2026-09-02); xAI Grok 4.6 = 156.44; frontier = 167.33. The gaps are 10.58 and 10.89, not 'both about 10.5'. Kimi K3 = 157.45. Google's best scored model, Gemini 3.7 Flash, is 157.27 and sits in the same tier. (c) I recomputed, per org, the best model dated on or before T within 6 ECI of that date's frontier (Google/Google DeepMind merged). 2025-09-30: OpenAI, Anthropic (3.16), xAI (3.56), Google (4.68), DeepSeek (5.0) = 5 orgs. 2026-06-30: OpenAI, Anthropic (0.01) = 2. 2026-09-30: Anthropic, OpenAI (0.88) = 2. All of this checks out. The 6-point threshold is arbitrary, though: Google's best at 2026-06 (Gemini 3.1 Pro, 154.77) is 7.3 behind. Gemini 4 Argon was announced 2026-09-30 with limited access, reportedly ranks first on Arena text with high reasoning and ties GPT-6 Astra on the AA index, and has no ECI score. So 'only 2' holds only within the set of ECI-scored models, and the conclusion is fragile. (d) The arXiv 2403.05812 abstract reads word for word: 'the compute required to reach a set performance threshold has halved approximately every 8 months, with a 95% confidence interval of around 5 to 14 months'. Scope: pretraining language modeling (WikiText/PTB perplexity), 2012–2023. (e) arXiv 2511.21622 reads word for word: 'Algorithms have been estimated to increase AI training FLOP efficiency by a factor of 22,000 ... we are able to account for less than 10× of these gains' and 'measures of algorithmic efficiency are strongly reference-dependent'. The 91% figure comes from 'Of the total measured progress of 21,400× ... 846× is achieved through LSTMs to Kaplan Transformers, and nearly 10× is attributable to Chinchilla rebalancing. Together, these comprise 91%'. (f) Epoch Gradient Updates (2026-03-23, newsletter commentary) reads: 'OpenAI spent $5 billion on R&D compute in 2024' and '9.6% for OpenAI, 22.6% for MiniMax, and 12.3% for Z.ai'. The quoted sentence opens with a condition, 'if most of the spending is exploration rather than execution, then a competitor who learns what works from the frontier could replicate the results for a fraction of the original cost'. The piece also says 'with only three data points, the evidence remains inconclusive'.

来源:https://epoch.ai/data/benchmark_data.zip (epoch_capabilities_index/eci_scores.csv, downloaded 2026-10-12) ; arXiv 2403.05812 PDF ; arXiv 2511.21622 PDF ; https://epoch.ai/gradient-updates/r-and-d-vs-training-compute

# G26 — 票型 ['CORRECTED', 'CORRECTED', 'CORRECTED']

## 票 1:CORRECTED(单源已核)

**修正后表述**:Epoch's 'The plunging price of thought' (2026-09-22) finds that, across five math, science and game benchmarks, the cost of reaching a given performance level has fallen about 47% per quarter since 2023 (about 13x per year). The decline is about 66% per quarter right after a level becomes SOTA and about 32% per quarter two years later. Among the eight traces in the figure, only one has an open-weights model as the 'second dot'. Cottier et al. find that the price at fixed benchmark performance falls 5–10x per year, while the cost of running frontier models on GPQA-D, SWE-bench V and AIME rises 3–18x per year. Snell et al. (2024) find that under FLOPs-matched comparison, on problems where the small model already has a non-trivial success rate, test-time compute can beat a model 14x larger. Toby Ord estimates from OpenAI's charts that 'a 10x scaling of RL is required to get the same performance boost as a 3x scaling of inference'.

**口径修正**:
- (a) All three figures and the quote are verbatim and HOLD. Add the scope: five benchmarks (math, sciences, games), and the faster decline at SOTA appears in only 3 of 5 benchmarks. 66% per quarter means 75x per year.
- (b) Verbatim HOLDS. The 3–18x per year is the cost of running the frontier model on three benchmarks (GPQA-D, SWE-bench V, AIME), not a per-token price.
- (c) Verbatim HOLDS. Keep the qualifiers 'FLOPs-matched' and 'on problems where the smaller model already has a non-trivial success rate'. The setup is PaLM 2-S* on MATH. The paper also finds that on the hardest problems pretraining compute is more effective.
- (d) Verbatim HOLDS. Label it as Ord's rough estimate from reading OpenAI's charts, not a controlled experiment.

**核对过程**:(a) Epoch report, dated Sep. 22, 2026 (Emberson & Roodman), updated Sep. 23 to add a caveat about how ambiguous the concept of 'price of thought' is. Verbatim: 'fallen about 47% per quarter since 2023, or 13× per year'; 'cost falls 66% per quarter (75× per year) for performance that has just debuted as SOTA. Two years later, prices fall half as fast, at 32% per quarter (4.7× per year)'; 'In only one of the eight traces in the figure does the model responsible for the second dot have open weights (QwQ-32B for 25% performance on AIME).' The scope is five primary benchmarks covering math, hard sciences and games of skill. The SOTA-premium pattern appears in only 3 of the 5 primary benchmarks. (b) The 2511.23455 abstract reads verbatim: 'the price for a given level of benchmark performance has decreased remarkably fast, around 5× to 10× per year, for frontier models on knowledge, reasoning, math, and software engineering benchmarks' and 'the price of running frontier models is rising between 3× to 18× per year due to bigger models and larger reasoning demands'. Section 5 defines this as the cost of running the best-performing model on GPQA-Diamond, SWE-bench Verified and AIME. (c) The 2408.03314 abstract reads verbatim: 'in a FLOPs-matched evaluation, we find that on problems where a smaller base model attains somewhat non-trivial success rates, test-time compute can be used to outperform a 14× larger model.' The setup is PaLM 2-S* on MATH. (d) On tobyord.com the original reads verbatim: 'a 10x scaling of RL is required to get the same performance boost as a 3x scaling of inference'. This is Ord's estimate from reading OpenAI's o1/o3 charts, a blog post and non-peer-reviewed preprint.

来源:https://epoch.ai/publications/the-plunging-price-of-thought ; arXiv PDF 2511.23455 ; arXiv PDF 2408.03314 ; https://www.tobyord.com/writing/how-well-does-rl-scale (and the EA Forum mirror)

## 票 2:CORRECTED(单源已核)

**修正后表述**:Epoch ('The plunging price of thought', 2026-09-22) estimates that the cost of a given level of performance has fallen about 47% per quarter (about 13x per year) since 2023. On average the decline is 66% per quarter right after a level becomes SOTA and 32% per quarter two years later (the pattern shows on 3 of 5 benchmarks). Of the eight traces in its figure, only one has an open-weight model as the second dot. Gundlach, Lynch, Mertens and Thompson ('The Price of Progress', arXiv 2511.23455) find that the price of fixed benchmark performance falls 5–10x per year, while 'the price of running frontier models is rising between 3× to 18× per year'. Snell et al. find that with FLOPs matched, on problems where a small model already has a non-trivial success rate, test-time compute 'can be used to outperform a 14x larger model'. From OpenAI's charts, Toby Ord estimates that 'a 10x scaling of RL is required to get the same performance boost as a 3x scaling of inference'.

**口径修正**:
- (a) Holds; can add that the SOTA-acceleration pattern appears on 3 of 5 benchmarks, and the open-weights observation is limited to the 8 traces in that figure
- (b) Kill attribution: 'Cottier et al.' → Gundlach, Lynch, Mertens, Thompson (MIT FutureTech) 'The Price of Progress'; numbers and quotes hold
- (c) Holds; must keep 'FLOPs-matched' and 'small model already has non-trivial success rate'
- (d) Holds; note it is Ord's back-of-envelope from OpenAI's published charts (o1/o3)

**核对过程**:(a) Epoch original text, 2026-09-22, Emberson and Roodman: 'fallen about 47% per quarter since 2023, or 13× per year'. 'Averaging across all five, cost falls 66% per quarter (75× per year) for performance that has just debuted as SOTA. Two years later... 32% per quarter (4.7× per year)'. The text also says 'We see this pattern on three of our five main benchmarks'. 'In only one of the eight traces in the figure does the model responsible for the second dot have open weights (QwQ-32B for 25% performance on AIME)' is verbatim, followed by the authors' qualification that competition between closed models may still be 'abetted by pressure from open ones'. All of these hold. (b) The abstract of arXiv 2511.23455 contains both verbatim phrases: 'around 5× to 10× per year' and 'the price of running frontier models is rising between 3× to 18× per year'. But the authors are Gundlach, Lynch, Mertens and Thompson (MIT FutureTech), not 'Cottier et al.' (Cottier wrote Epoch's earlier inference-price work). The same abstract also estimates algorithmic efficiency at about 3x per year. (c) Snell abstract verbatim: 'in a FLOPs-matched evaluation, we find that on problems where a smaller base model attains somewhat non-trivial success rates, test-time compute can be used to outperform a 14x larger model'. Holds with the qualifier kept. (d) Ord original text verbatim: 'a 10x scaling of RL is required to get the same performance boost as a 3x scaling of inference'. It derives from OpenAI's o1/o3 charts, is a back-of-envelope estimate, and is not independently replicated.

来源:https://epoch.ai/publications/the-plunging-price-of-thought (full HTML downloaded and parsed) ; arXiv 2511.23455 abstract page ; arXiv 2408.03314 abstract ; https://www.tobyord.com/writing/how-well-does-rl-scale

## 票 3:CORRECTED(单源已核)

**修正后表述**:Epoch's 'The plunging price of thought' (2026-09-22) finds that the cost of a given level of AI performance has fallen about 47% per quarter since 2023 (about 13x a year). It falls about 66% per quarter right after first reaching SOTA and about 32% per quarter two years later. The report notes that in only one of the eight traces in the figure does the model responsible for the second dot have open weights. Gundlach et al. (MIT FutureTech, 'The Price of Progress', arXiv 2511.23455) find that the price of a fixed level of benchmark performance falls 5–10x a year, while 'the price of running frontier models is rising between 3× to 18× per year'. Snell et al. (2024) find that, FLOPs-matched, on problems where the smaller model already has some success rate, test-time compute 'can be used to outperform a 14× larger model'. Toby Ord estimates from OpenAI's public charts that 'a 10x scaling of RL is required to get the same performance boost as a 3x scaling of inference'.

**口径修正**:
- (a) HOLDS
- (b) Wrong attribution: 'Cottier et al. The Price of Progress' → Gundlach, Lynch, Mertens, Thompson (MIT FutureTech), arXiv 2511.23455. The figures and quotes are correct. Add: '3–18x' is the rising price of running frontier models on benchmarks, driven by larger models and longer reasoning
- (c) HOLDS. Add: PaLM 2-S* on MATH, FLOPs-matched; the advantage depends on the ratio of inference to pretraining load, and pretraining wins on the hardest questions
- (d) HOLDS. Label it Ord's heuristic estimate from OpenAI's public charts

**核对过程**:(a) The Epoch report (datePublished 2026-09-22, Emberson & Roodman) reads word for word: 'fallen about 47% per quarter since 2023, or 13× per year'; 'cost falls 66% per quarter (75× per year) for performance that has just debuted as SOTA. Two years later, prices fall half as fast, at 32% per quarter (4.7× per year)'. The report's own Discussion section has: 'In only one of the eight traces in the figure does the model responsible for the second dot have open weights (QwQ-32B for 25% performance on AIME)'. Recomputation: 0.53^4 → 12.7x per year, which checks out. (b) arXiv 2511.23455 'The Price of Progress' is authored by Hans Gundlach, Jayson Lynch, Matthias Mertens and Neil Thompson (MIT FutureTech), v2 dated 2026-03-23. It is not by Cottier et al., who are only cited inside it for '10–1,000×'. The quotes match verbatim: 'decreased remarkably fast, around 5× to 10× per year, for frontier models on knowledge, reasoning, math, and software engineering benchmarks' and 'the price of running frontier models is rising between 3× to 18× per year due to bigger models and larger reasoning demands'. (c) The Snell abstract reads verbatim: 'on problems where a smaller base model attains somewhat non-trivial success rates, test-time compute can be used to outperform a 14× larger model'. The body adds that this holds for easy and medium questions, depends on the ratio of inference to pretraining load, and that pretraining is more effective on the hardest questions (PaLM 2-S*, MATH). (d) Toby Ord's EA Forum / blog text reads verbatim: 'a 10x scaling of RL is required to get the same performance boost as a 3x scaling of inference'. He infers this from public OpenAI o1→o3→GPT-5 charts, so it is a heuristic, not an experiment.

来源:https://epoch.ai/publications/the-plunging-price-of-thought ; arXiv 2511.23455v2 PDF ; arXiv 2408.03314 PDF ; https://www.tobyord.com/writing/how-well-does-rl-scale (+ EA Forum version)

# G27 — 票型 ['CORRECTED', 'CORRECTED', 'CORRECTED']

## 票 1:CORRECTED(单源已核)

**修正后表述**:The leaked memo from a Google researcher published by SemiAnalysis on 2023-05-04 said the open-source gap 'is closing astonishingly quickly'. SignalFire's May 2025 report found engineers were 8 times more likely to move from OpenAI to Anthropic than the reverse, with two-year retention of 80% at Anthropic, 78% at DeepMind and 67% at OpenAI (Meta as a whole: 64%). The FT reported in April 2025 that DeepMind had imposed up to a six-month embargo on strategic generative-AI papers (secondary relay; DeepMind said it still publishes widely). Meta's Muse Spark (2026-04-08) did not release weights, and Meta itself acknowledged 'current performance gaps, such as long-horizon agentic systems and coding workflows'.

**口径修正**:
- (a) Verbatim HOLDS. Describe it as a leaked memo from one Google researcher, not Google's position.
- (b) Verbatim HOLDS (May 2025 data). Meta's 64% is company-wide retention (described as FAANG), not Meta's AI lab. Note the data predates the 2025 talent moves at Meta Superintelligence Labs.
- (c) The FT original is unverified; only consistent secondary relays exist. Keep the wording 'per FT reporting (media relay)' and attach DeepMind's response that it still publishes hundreds of papers a year.
- (d) HOLDS. Meta acknowledged 'current performance gaps, such as long-horizon agentic systems and coding workflows'. Weights not released, private API preview. Note that 1.1 to 1.3 later targeted these gaps, while ECI still puts it about 10.6 below the frontier.

**核对过程**:(a) SemiAnalysis, May 04, 2023, by Dylan Patel and Afzal Ahmad. The body contains, verbatim: 'While our models still hold a slight edge in terms of quality, the gap is closing astonishingly quickly.' The piece describes the document as coming from 'a researcher within Google', leaked through Discord, so it is one employee's view, not the company's position. (b) SignalFire State of Tech Talent Report, published May 20, 2025. Verbatim: 'Engineers are 8 times more likely to leave OpenAI for Anthropic than the reverse.' 'An impressive 80% of employees hired at least 2 years ago at Anthropic were still at the company at the end of their second year... DeepMind follows closely at 78%, while OpenAI's retention trails at 67% but remains on par with large FAANG companies like Meta (64%).' The Meta figure is company-wide FAANG retention, not an AI-lab figure. The data predates Meta's large-scale AI hiring push from mid-2025. (c) I could not reach the original FT article. Several secondary reports (xatakaon, slashdot 2025-04-01, itdaily) consistently describe a six-month embargo on 'strategic' generative-AI papers and quote DeepMind's response. It remains media relay, as originally framed. (d) The Meta blog 'Introducing Muse Spark', dated April 8, 2026, says verbatim: 'We continue to invest in areas with current performance gaps, such as long-horizon agentic systems and coding workflows.' The release was a private API preview with no weights, and Epoch lists it as 'API access'. The blog does not explicitly say 'closed-source', but no weights were released, so 'closed' stands.

来源:https://newsletter.semianalysis.com/p/google-we-have-no-moat-and-neither ; https://www.signalfire.com/blog/signalfire-state-of-talent-report-2025 ; https://ai.meta.com/blog/introducing-muse-spark-msl/ ; FT original not reachable (secondary: xatakaon, slashdot, itdaily)

## 票 2:CORRECTED(单源已核)

**修正后表述**:A leaked internal document by a Google researcher, published by SemiAnalysis on 2023-05-04, wrote that 'the gap is closing astonishingly quickly'. SignalFire's 2025 report found 'Engineers are 8 times more likely to leave OpenAI for Anthropic than the reverse', with two-year retention of about 80% at Anthropic, 78% at DeepMind, 67% at OpenAI and 64% at Meta (small samples, 2025 data). The FT reported in spring 2025 that DeepMind set a six-month embargo on strategic generative-AI papers (per media coverage; DeepMind says it still publishes). Meta's Muse Spark launched 2026-04-08 with closed weights, and Meta acknowledged gaps in long-horizon agents and coding at the time. Subsequent versions 1.1 and 1.3 targeted that gap (Meta self-reports near-parity), but weights remain closed and on Epoch ECI it is still about 10.6 points below the frontier.

**口径修正**:
- (a) Holds on the quote; must state 'a leaked document by an individual Google researcher', not Google's official position
- (b) Holds; add that the 80% is defined as 'employees hired at least 2 years ago, still there at end of year 2', the data are 2025, and SignalFire itself notes small startup samples make retention hard to assess
- (c) Holds as media reporting; keep 'FT reported / per media' and DeepMind's response
- (d) Holds for the April 2026 launch; must add the time point: 1.1/1.3 targeted agentic coding (Meta self-reports near-parity on DeepSWE, not independently verified), weights still closed, and ECI still about 10.6 below the frontier

**核对过程**:(a) SemiAnalysis original text: 'While our models still hold a slight edge in terms of quality, the gap is closing astonishingly quickly.' The editor's note says the document 'originates from a researcher within Google', so it reflects an individual researcher's view, not Google's official position. The article must not call it 'Google's memo'. (b) SignalFire original text: 'Engineers are 8 times more likely to leave OpenAI for Anthropic than the reverse', plus 'nearly 11:1' from DeepMind. Retention: Anthropic 80%, DeepMind 78%, OpenAI 67%, Meta 64%, with the 80% defined as 'employees hired at least 2 years ago'. A counter-reading: SignalFire's own August 2025 analysis notes that the small headcount and short history of these startups make retention hard to assess, and that Meta was the best Big Tech performer. The data are from 2025 and predate Meta's 2025 hiring spree and the 2026 personnel changes. (c) We could not access the FT original. Secondary coverage (Slashdot 2025-04-01 etc.) consistently reports 'a six-month embargo before strategic papers related to generative AI are released'. DeepMind responded that it still publishes and contributes to the research ecosystem. Keep it at the level of media reporting. (d) Muse Spark launched 2026-04-08, the first MSL model, with weights closed. Several secondary sources quote the Meta blog: 'We continue to invest in areas with current performance gaps, specifically long-horizon agentic systems and coding workflows.' I could not open Meta's original page. Counter-check: Muse Spark 1.1 (07-09) and 1.3 (09-02) targeted this gap; Meta self-reports DeepSWE 75.4 vs Claude Opus 5 at 74.0, unverified independently. Weights remain closed, with open weights only said to be 'on the roadmap'. Epoch ECI puts Muse Spark 1.3 at 156.75, still about 10.6 below the frontier.

来源:https://newsletter.semianalysis.com/p/google-we-have-no-moat-and-neither ; https://www.signalfire.com/blog/signalfire-state-of-talent-report-2025 ; FT second-hand coverage (tech.slashdot.org/story/25/04/01/1648250, itdaily) ; Muse Spark coverage (charonhub.deeplearning.ai, decrypt, infoworld, verdent quoting the Meta blog)

## 票 3:CORRECTED(单源已核)

**修正后表述**:In a leaked internal memo published by SemiAnalysis on 2023-05-04, an anonymous Google researcher wrote 'the gap is closing astonishingly quickly'. SignalFire's 2025 talent report finds that engineers are 8 times more likely to leave OpenAI for Anthropic than the reverse, and gives two-year retention of 80% at Anthropic, 78% at DeepMind, 67% at OpenAI and 64% at Meta. The FT reported in April 2025, citing anonymous sources, that DeepMind had set a six-month embargo on 'strategic' generative AI papers (secondary-source account). Meta released the closed-weight Muse Spark on 2026-04-08 and acknowledged in its launch post 'current performance gaps, such as long-horizon agentic systems and coding workflows'. By September, version 1.3 still trailed the ECI frontier by about 10.6 points.

**口径修正**:
- (a) HOLDS. Label it a leaked memo from an anonymous Google researcher, not Google's official position
- (b) HOLDS. Add the source and date (SignalFire, 2025-05) and the definition: two-year retention among employees hired at least 2 years earlier
- (c) Downgrade to 'FT report (anonymous sources), not verified first-hand'. DeepMind has not admitted a 'ban' and says it still preserves publishing
- (d) HOLDS. Date the admission to the April 2026 launch post. Note that 1.2/1.3 later targeted coding and agents, and that by ECI the gap is still about 10.6

**核对过程**:(a) SemiAnalysis datePublished 2023-05-04, verbatim: 'While our models still hold a slight edge in terms of quality, the gap is closing astonishingly quickly'. This is a leaked internal memo from an anonymous Google researcher, not Google's official position. (b) The SignalFire report (published 2025-05-20) reads verbatim: 'Engineers are 8 times more likely to leave OpenAI for Anthropic than the reverse. From DeepMind, the ratio is nearly 11:1'. Also: '80% of employees hired at least 2 years ago at Anthropic were still at the company at the end of their second year ... DeepMind follows closely at 78%, while OpenAI's retention trails at 67% ... Meta (64%)'. These come from SignalFire's proprietary talent data. (c) I could not access the FT original. Several secondary outlets consistently report that FT (spring 2025, anonymous current and former employees) described a six-month embargo on 'strategic' generative AI papers. DeepMind replied that it still preserves the ability to publish. (d) Meta's blog is dated April 8, 2026 and reads verbatim: 'We continue to invest in areas with current performance gaps, such as long-horizon agentic systems and coding workflows'. It released only meta.ai and a private API preview, with no weights; Epoch lists Muse Spark as API access. Meta's later 1.2 (2026-08) and 1.3 (2026-09-02) releases targeted coding and agents, and 1.3 scores 156.75 on ECI, still about 10.6 behind the frontier.

来源:https://newsletter.semianalysis.com/p/google-we-have-no-moat-and-neither ; SignalFire State of Tech Talent Report 2025 (signalfire.com) ; https://ai.meta.com/blog/introducing-muse-spark-msl/ ; FT original not accessed (paywall), only secondary coverage (sherwood.news, slashdot, itdaily)
